V-Synth VariPhrase Emulator — Quick Reference
==============================================
Last updated: Session 16 (2026-10-18) — XT SOLO records; v34 epoch encoder

Current score: 73.9 / 100 under METRIC v6 — v35 (SOLO encoder epochs matched to the XT's own records, sub-sample; vocal 74.6 -> 77.4) (v28 = 66.6 v6 / 68.1 v5 / 52.5 v4) (v17 baseline = 34.0)
  METRIC v4 (commit 7434abb): phase-blind spectral similarity (MR-STFT +
  log-mel, gain-matched) carries most of the composite; content-aware weights
  (speech/percussive/tonal by filename); SNR rescaled to 30 dB full marks.
  Rationale in PROJECT_REVIEW.md: the V-Synth resynthesizes, so phase-
  sensitive null testing cannot measure perceptual closeness. HTML batch
  report now embeds A/B audio players — LISTEN, then read numbers.
  METRIC v3 (commit eb18467): optimal-gain null test. v17 under v3 = 27.5.
  METRIC v2 (commit 3ecdfdf): compare.py time-aligns ref/output via envelope
  cross-correlation. Pre-v2 scores are not comparable (8 of 20 pairs were
  scored against reference lead-in silence). v17 baseline under v2 = 27.4.
  20 cases: 6 sine + 7 vocal-aah + 4 drum + 3 chord
  Best single case: vocal_aah_time_2x = 57.7
Best version: v28 — v27 + subband-stretched tails in the event-based BACKING
  path (drum_2x 51.9->57.2, halfspeed 49.6->52.1, 4x 67.8->64.7, net +4.7).
  ENSEMBLE pitch via subband tested: 39.6 vs 40.1 PV — not adopted.
  UNIT TESTS: plugin/Source/EngineTests.cpp (-DENGINE_TESTS_MAIN), 10 checks,
  ALL PASS. Known edge: bit-exact periodic input (no noise floor)
  destabilises per-frame F0 (spurious 472 Hz at -4 dB on perfect 440 sine);
  real captures are unaffected. Git tags: v25..v28.
v27 — v26 + SUBBAND TIME STRETCH for ENSEMBLE time-only, per
  Roland US6564187B1 (quarter-octave analytic sub-bands ≈ one partial each,
  amplitude/inst-freq trajectories resampled, cosine-bank resynthesis;
  identity check 0.958). chord_Cmaj_time_2x 39.3 -> 62.7.
  v26b: patent grain read offset (os = cwp - ppw*fsv on window clamp).
v26 (commit 1bd670c) — PITCH-SYNCHRONOUS GRANULAR ENGINE for
  SOLO content, implementing Roland's actual VariPhrase patent US6421642B1
  (see research/PATENTS.md). Encode cuts pitch-period grains; playback:
  time = grain-list walk rate, pitch = grain re-trigger period (2 channels,
  half-cycle offset, triangular windows), formant = grain read velocity with
  the patent's wl<=ppw window clamp (replaces the 0.75x calibration).
  SOLO only: granular lifted every vocal case but dropped every sine case —
  LITE is Roland's reduced-analysis mode, keeps v23 routing. v24b's dsLPC
  pitch path removed (obsolete: vocal pitch no longer reaches SFM; it was
  quietly costing sine_pitch_up7st 62.3->23.9 on re-render).
v25 (commit df565b6) — v24b + event-based stretch for BACKING
  (onset stamps at encode time; attacks placed verbatim at stretched
  positions, inter-event material PV-stretched; attack 2048 samples).
  BACKING threshold raised peakToMean 5 -> 9 (chord 7.8 = ENSEMBLE,
  drum 11.4 = BACKING).
v24b (commit c11cc1f) — v24 + downsampled envelope on the
  vocal PITCH path too (ratio 1.0; vocal_aah_pitch_up7st 26.7 -> 36.1).
v24 (commit 74620ca) — v23 + downsampled formant LPC for
  speech (decimate x4 -> order-12 LPC at ~12 kHz -> poles mapped back theta/4,
  r^0.25 -> shifted biquads; autocorrelation smoothed alpha=0.7 across frames;
  real/tilt poles dropped; polyphonic content excluded) + formant-knob
  calibration (V-Synth upward mapping saturates ~0.75x nominal; downward
  tracks nominal — measured from references).
v23 (commit 80e0c3b) — v22b + LITE content routes to LPC
  resynthesis for time-only and pitch-UP ops. KEY ARCHITECTURE FINDING: the
  V-Synth RESYNTHESIZES pure tones even for time stretch (its sine references
  carry sawtooth harmonics the dry input lacks); transparent stretching can
  never match. Pitch-DOWN stays on PV (LPC envelope resonant at original F0
  boosts the wrong harmonic: 6.1 LPC vs 48.8 PV).
v22b (commit ff8ecea) — output-domain energy normalization (frame energy
  targeted in the SIGNAL domain via fresh-state de-emphasis estimate;
  rejected under metric v2, wins under v3).
v22 (commit eb18467) — v21 + WSOLA routing disabled (PV beats
  WSOLA everywhere after the overhaul; drum halfspeed 31.1 PV vs 26.0 WSOLA)
  + metric v3.
v21 (commit 4fb2a56) — v20 (flat excitation) + PV overhaul:
  inverted hops (synthesis hop fixed at kHopSize, analysis hop =
  round(kHopSize/stretch) integer), synthesis Hann window, identity phase
  locking (Laroche & Dolson 1999), FFT SIGN FIX (forward FFT uses +j —
  phases advance by -omega*hop; legacy recursion only worked at hop 512 =
  N/4 where the sign error self-cancelled on even bins).
  Pure tones now land exactly on target at every stretch/pitch ratio.
  Outputs: analysis/plugin_outputs/hybrid_v18d  (dir name lags version; scores in
  analysis/results/hybrid_v18d)

CRITICAL Session 15 fixes (see RESEARCH_LOG Session 15 for the full story):
  1. processOffline drain mode: output = inputLen×stretch; rings pre-sized
     (setOutputCapacity); zero-input drain after feed. Old renders corrupted
     after 1.37 s on extension (ring lap).
  2. OLA read gating: reader never passes writer (outputAvail_); per-call
     valid prefix (lastValidOutput_) compacted by processOffline. Compression
     no longer loses frames behind the read pointer.
  3. OLA-coherent excitation: sawPhase_ now advances by the SYNTHESIS HOP per
     frame, not the full frame. The old full-frame advance made overlapping
     frames phase-incoherent (10 dB cancellation) — masked for 13 sessions by
     the pre-gating reader reading partial sums + one-lap-late leftovers
     (accidental 0.37 s echo). DO NOT revert gating to chase the old vocal
     formant scores (34.x) — those were metric artifacts of the echo.
  4. RENDER RATIOS: time_2x → --time 0.5, time_4x → 0.25, halfspeed → 2.0
     (verified against reference content durations). Earlier sessions rendered
     the wrong direction.

Routing (v18g: pitch→LPC now requires SOLO content type):
  hasFormant (|formantShift| > 0.5 st)              → LPC source-filter
  hasPitch AND SOLO AND voiced speech (2 kHz bp)    → LPC source-filter
  else                                               → Phase Vocoder
  (chord = BACKING content; its blocks were transiently passing the per-block
   2 kHz check and routing to LPC — SOLO gate fixed chord_Cmaj_pitch_up7st)
  LPC min guard order = 8 when voiced AND (hasFormant OR hasPitch) (else 2)

Pre-emphasis logic (Sessions 13-14, the v18 series):
  H(z) = 1 - 0.97z^-1 applied to LPC analysis frame. v18h: DUAL OLA STREAMS --
  pre-emphasised frames OLA into outputBuffer_ (de-emphasised continuously at
  read time); bypassed frames OLA into outputBufferPlain_ (read as-is); summed
  per sample at output. Direction-aware bypass:
    formantUpBypass: voiced AND formant shift >= 0 (v18h: now includes speech)
      -> use plain frame. Pre-emph distorts upward pole shifts
      (sine_440_formant_upmax: 32.8 plain vs 13.7 pre-emph, old metric;
      vocal_aah_formant_upmax recovered to 34.5 once dual streams removed the
      cross-tilt), but helps downward shifts -> keep pre-emph for those.
    largePitchDown: voiced AND no formant shift AND pitch < -6 st
      -> use plain frame (vocal_aah_pitch_down12st 27.0 plain vs 19.9 pre-emph,
      old metric); upward pitch keeps pre-emph (vocal_aah_pitch_up7st 24.9 vs 19.0).
    polyphonicContent (VariphraseParams flag, set by Hybrid routing for
      ENSEMBLE/BACKING): bypassed frames route into the DE-EMPH stream anyway --
      chord formant empirically wants the low-shelf tilt (21.2 tilted vs 17.9
      clean vs 19.8 pre-emph, old metric).
  DO NOT fold de-emphasis into the LPC polynomial (A(z)*(1-0.97z^-1)): order
  P+1 truncated to P distorts ALL formant roots (v18d failure).
  DO NOT de-emphasise per-frame with fresh state (v18 experiment, 27.0):
  the integrator needs cross-frame state at F0 ~ 120 Hz; dual streams with
  continuous state per stream is the working design.

(Old-metric history, not comparable: v10b=26.9 → v17c=27.2 → v18e-g=27.4-27.7 → v18h=28.0)

SOLO encoder (v39): plugin/Source/XtSoloEncoder.cpp needs one table from the
  user's XT firmware image — set VSE_XT_ROM=<path to V-SynthXT.rom.2.bin> or
  keep the unpacked copy at firmware/unpacked/ (gitignored; the renderer looks
  there relative to the working directory). Without it the v38 epoch encoder
  is used. VSE_ENC=epoch|vt forces the older encoders.
  Stereo sources: the XT encodes (L+R) >> 1, so the engine floors its int16
  conversion of the 0.5·(L+R) mono input; `xt_encode rom samp.bin stereo`
  takes interleaved L/R.

BACKING / ENSEMBLE players (2026-10-28): plugin/Source/XtMultiBandPlayer.cpp,
  bit-exact against the firmware players run in the P-code emulator. Like
  the SOLO encoder it reads its tables from the user's rom.2 at run time.
  Not wired into the engine yet: it needs the load-time band data and the
  vpdt, which come from the multi-band encoder (not ported). Validation CLI:
    clang++ -std=c++20 -O2 -ffp-contract=off -DXT_MBPLAYER_MAIN \
      plugin/Source/XtMultiBandPlayer.cpp -o xt_mbplay
    xt_mbplay rom.2.bin vpdt.bin mem.samp b1,..,b5 <ch> out.f32 <pitch> <walk>
  (inputs as research/ghidra_scripts/EmuBacking.java; XT_MODE=5 ENSEMBLE,
  XT_ROUND=nearest to match the emulator's rounding).
    xt_mbplay rom.2.bin --bands samp.bin <ch> out.samp
  rebuilds a BACKING wave's band data (xtMbBuildBacking) as the XT's sample
  memory and prints the band addresses, for comparison with EmuLoad.java.

SH-4 interpreter (2026-10-28): plugin/Source/Sh4Interp.cpp runs rom.2 code;
  plugin/Source/XtFirmware.cpp wraps it (xtFwSoloEncode, xtFwLoadBands).
  CLI:
    clang++ -std=c++20 -O2 -ffp-contract=off -DXT_FW_MAIN plugin/Source/XtFirmware.cpp \
      plugin/Source/Sh4Interp.cpp plugin/Source/XtMultiBandPlayer.cpp plugin/Source/XtSoloVoice.cpp -o xt_fw
    xt_fw rom.2.bin solo samp.bin                       (SOLO records)
    xt_fw rom.2.bin load <3|5> samp.bin <ch> out        (band data)
    xt_fw rom.2.bin play <3|5> samp.bin <ch> vpdt.bin <pitch> <walk> out.f32
  XT_ROUND=nearest rounds to nearest (Ghidra's emulator) instead of FPSCR.RM.

Multi-band (v41): ENSEMBLE content renders through the XT's own chain
  (VariphraseEngine::multiBandFirmwareOffline): resample to 44.1 kHz,
  firmware encoder (xtFwEncodeMulti) and band rebuild (xtFwLoadBands) in the
  SH-4 interpreter, ported player (XtMultiBandPlayer), resample back. Needs
  the ROM (as the SOLO encoder). v42: BACKING content too, and the markers
  come from the XT's own attack detection at the Encode Depth (VSE_DEPTH,
  default 64; −1 = start and end markers only). VSE_MB=off: the v40 paths;
  VSE_MB=ensemble: BACKING on the v40 paths.

SOLO v44 (2026-10-29): stereo SOLO waves play both channels through one
  record stream (0x8950d1f0); unvoiced records play at S = 1 (info low
  bits gate A/F); grain parameters in single precision; the second tap
  clamps at the end. Port vs emulator −101…−118 dB (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md,
  "Stereo SOLO playback"). Validation CLI, same inputs as EmuSolo.java:
    clang++ -std=c++20 -O2 -DXT_SOLO_MAIN plugin/Source/XtSoloVoice.cpp -o xt_solo
    xt_solo cuts.txt samp.bin <1|2> <R> <F> <walk> [energy] [maxOut] out.f32
  (XT_ROUND=nearest to match the emulator; VSE_TRACE=file traces as EmuSolo;
  EMUSOLO_CH=2 makes EmuSolo.java play L/R pairs.)

ANALOG OSC v79 (2026-11-01): OSC Type ANALOG with SAW (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md,
  "The analog oscillator, and SAW"). XtAnalog: XtAnalogParams, xtAnalogBlock
  (converter 0x894e1920: PW 0x894e0ea0, Fat 0x894e1010, level 0x894e1350,
  slope 0x894e14a0), XtAnalogOsc (note-on 0x89504af0, update 0x89505400, SAW
  0x89508c20); the hidden calibration (IDs 0x528–0x55b) from the descriptors'
  defaults. xt_analog checks it (fcheck); PORT=analog mutate.sh. XtSynth:
  Controls.oscType (0 ANALOG, 1 PCM) and .ana; a voice's analog flag; pitch
  against A4, no glide; PW on the time envelope, Fat on the formant one;
  plays without a sample. Plugin params oscType, anaWave, anaImpact, anaPw…
  anaFatEnv; the editor's ANALOG rows replace Encode/VariPhrase/Loop.
  XT_ANALOG / TEST_ANALOG "wave,impact,pw,pwKf,pwLfo,pwEnv,fat,fatKf,fatLfo,
  fatEnv" (KFs in %); TEST_SNAPSHOT_ANALOG=1.

ANALOG WAVEFORMS v80 (2026-11-01): SQUARE, TRIANGLE, SINE, RAMP, JUNO, NOISE
  (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The analog waveforms"). XtAnalogOsc::process picks
  the routine by code (saw/square/triangle/sine/ramp/juno/noise) and returns
  the wrap mask (OSC SYNC's source; SAW's too: v79's "address" was wrong).
  shape() forms the Pulse Width by code, SQUARE's edge (+0x1e0) and NOISE's
  hold (+0x248); NOISE keeps its second state (+0x244) across notes.
  xtAnalogPlays: codes 2…8. xt_analog runs the install (0x89505170) and the
  routine it puts at +0x968, seven waveforms in turn; breakpoints() gives
  each routine's points for its exact-phase cases. Left: HQ-SAW/HQ-SQUARE
  and codes 9…13, the sub oscillator.

ANALOG HQ v81 (2026-11-01): HQ-SAW (0x895063f0) and HQ-SQUARE (0x895060e0)
  (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "HQ-SAW and HQ-SQUARE"): a leaky integrator
  (+0x1e4, hy) of a band-limited impulse from rom.2's tables A (0x897d94dc,
  ±4608) and B (0x897e5ce0, ±8192), read at run time (T.hq). hqFrame() forms
  the block's lengths and factors; HQ-SQUARE's direction is +0x1e8 (hs).
  xtAnalogPlays: codes 0…8. xt_analog checks them in a second pass ("hq"
  lines) so the other waveforms' lines stay. TEST_ANALOG's KFs must be
  multiples of 10 (%): the parameters step by 10.

ANALOG LA v82 (2026-11-01): LA-SQUARE (code 9, 0x89508540) and LA-SAW (code
  10, 0x895081f0) (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "LA-SQUARE and LA-SAW"), with the map
  of codes 9…13's note-on and update cases. laShape() is 0x89505950, for both
  the note-on and the update; laNoteOn() is 0x89504bf6. Their gain and Fat/PW
  targets are per sample (gainT = −gain·2^26). New state: laInc +0x1f4, ph2
  +0x218, qT/qC +0x224/+0x228. XtAnalogBlock tone, toneKf (+0xbc, +0xc0);
  T.toneKf from 0x897d4c6c. +0x20c is read and never written: taken as 0.
  xtAnalogPlays: codes 0…10. xt_analog: "la" and "la tones" lines, the latter
  with the calibration's Tones and KFs random through 0x894e5f80.

ANALOG SUPER-SAW v83 (2026-11-01): SUPER-SAW (code 11, 0x89507520)
  (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The analog waveform SUPER-SAW"). The converter has
  its own case: PW → the saws' spread (ssDetuneCurve, table 0x897cef58), the
  Detune knob → the side saws' level (ssMixCurve), and the Saw Slope (the
  switch 0x894e14a0 sends patch waveforms 11…13 to SAW's branch). ssShape() is
  the update 0x89505c90, ssNoteOn() 0x89505bc0. XtAnalogShared (one per
  XtSynth, so a noteOn parameter): the counter 0x8987c894 the six side phases
  start from, 0 at startup. New param anaDetune (XT_ANALOG/TEST_ANALOG's
  optional eleventh value). X-MOD (0x89507b60) reads OSC 2's buffer, so it
  waits for OSC 2.

ANALOG FEEDBACK-OSC v84 (2026-11-01): FEEDBACK-OSC (code 12, 0x89507900)
  (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The analog waveform FEEDBACK-OSC"). Converter case: PW
  → the second tap's place (fbTapCurve), the Detune knob → its level
  (fbLevelCurve). fbShape() is the update, fbNoteOn() 0x89504d30. XtAnalogShared
  holds the eighteen 2,700-byte lines and flags; release() is 0x895053c0, called
  from XtSynth::startVoice for stopped and stolen voices. It shares ssDet*/ssMix*
  (+0x24c…+0x258) with SUPER-SAW; fbZ +0x274, fbW +0x278, fbSlot +0x27c.
  xtAnalogPlays: codes 0…12. Only X-MOD and the sub oscillator remain.

ANALOG SUB OSCILLATOR v85 (2026-11-02): Sub Octave above 0 on patch waveforms 0…10
  (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The analog sub oscillator"). xtAnalogBlock: subOn +0xb4,
  subLevel +0xb8, fat +0x84 in octaves (subDetuneCurve, subCents). XtAnalogOsc latches
  subOn_ at note-on; subShape() is 0x89506bf0, subNoteOn() 0x89506700; subPeak (SAW,
  TRIANGLE, SINE), subSquare, subRamp, subJuno, subLaSquare, subLaSaw. HQ-SAW, HQ-SQUARE
  and NOISE keep their routines. New fields swT/swC +0x1ec/+0x1f0, subInc +0x1f8, sph
  +0x1fc, lvlT/lvlC +0x200/+0x204, rampK/rampK2 +0x208/+0x210 (rampK carries to later
  notes), ph3 +0x21c, q2T/q2C +0x22c/+0x230, sqT/sqC/sq2T/sq2C +0x234…+0x240. Params
  anaSubOct, anaSubLevel; XT_ANALOG/TEST_ANALOG's optional twelfth and thirteenth.
  Sh4Interp: SZ = 1 pair moves now put FR(n) at the lower address, as the SH7750
  does; only RAMP's sub routine depended on it.

OSC 2 v86 (2026-11-02): a second analog voice on each note, and the mix
  (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "OSC 2, and how the two OSCs are mixed"). XtSynth::Controls
  derives from OscControls (OSC 1's fields keep their names) and holds osc2, off by default;
  Voice derives from OscVoice with osc2. targets, gainTarget, noteCents, analogBlock,
  keyFollowKey, ampEnvParams and lfoParams take the OSC. Order: per-note setup and note-on
  OSC 1 then 2; render OSC 2 then 1. xtAnalogMix is the MOD off or MIX: the sum in OSC 1's
  buffer's units. An OSC that is off has ratio 1 and root 440. OSC 2 is ANALOG only:
  oscPlays gives PCM to OSC 1 alone. Sync runs OSC 2 → OSC 1 only with MOD Type OSC SYNC, so
  the port passes none. Params oscSwitch, osc2… (Params::oscId); editor attachOsc.
  XT_ANALOG2, XT_PENV2…; TEST_OSC2…. Left: X-MOD, PCM on OSC 2 (a wave per OSC, the rows, the
  stereo flag), MOD Types RING, FM, ENV RING, OSC SYNC, the other two Structure Types.

ANALOG X-MOD v87 (2026-11-02): X-MOD (code 13, 0x89507b60 at +0x96c)
  (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The analog waveform X-MOD"). Converter case: PW → the depth
  (xmDepthCurve), all 0 on OSC 2 (xtAnalogBlock's osc2), Fat as SAW's. xmShape() is 0x89505f10,
  xmNoteOn() 0x89505ec0; depth in ssDetT/ssDetC (+0x24c/+0x250). xMod(lr, sync, mod) moves the
  phase by (L/2^31 + R/2^31)/2 × 0.2·depth and clears mod; process(lr, sync, mod) passes it,
  XtSynth gives OSC 1 OSC 2's buffer in the XT's units and OSC 2 none (depth 0). The phase is
  brought back by 1 once only, so it can stay out of range. xtAnalogPlays: all fourteen. Left
  on the roadmap: MOD Types RING, FM, ENV RING, OSC SYNC; the other two Structure Types; the patch
  TVA's level and pan; PCM on OSC 2.

PCM ON OSC 2 v103 (2026-11-02): each OSC its own wave and encode (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "PCM on
  OSC 2, with its own wave"). XtSynth: setSample(e, osc), cur[2]; OscVoice::wave latched at note-on;
  waveLoop(s, o); pcmBlock in the mix, either OSC. A VariPhrase voice is kind 2: note-on 0x894f3110. Stereo
  flag: COSMs and TVA take OSC 1's (COSM 2 in Type3 OSC 2's), the TVA never reads it, the mono COSMs read L;
  the MOD now gets OSC 2's too. No analog voice has the flag, HQ-SQUARE included. Left: a stereo wave on OSC 1
  with a COSM on, or on OSC 2 with COSM 2 under Type3, needs the stereo routines; XtSoloVoice is close, not
  exact. Backward at high pitch the firmware's band reads leave the wave; not compared. Params

The Multi Step Modulator v192 (2026-11-11): the firmware's own, which 55 of the 512 preset patches use (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md,
  "The Multi Step Modulator"), ported and checked; not wired into the sound yet (v193). Numbered from v192 on its own branch
  (claude/msm-ad515c): v188 to v191 are the arpeggio's and the Algorithm menu's removal, on master.
  It is the object at 0x89cf7634, the one called "the matrix" before v119: four tracks of 16 steps on an OS tempo timer that
  fires every 20 of the 480 ticks a quarter, counted from its start. A track counts its Grid's clocks (24, 12, 8, 6, 4, 3: the
  8TH-T and 16TH-T are triplets, as the arpeggio's), steps by its Direction and makes its term: STEP its step, SMOOTH a line to
  the next step each clock. The converters add the terms of the tracks on their destination and zone to their knobs; the coarse
  tunes' through a curve, ±63 to ±24 semitones. Key Sync OFF starts it at the first note and never stops it, and every track
  then loops whatever its Loop Switch; PART restarts it at a note with no other key down; VOICE at every note; both stop it
  when no note of part 0 sounds. Part 0's only.
  Code: XtMsm.h, XtMsm.cpp (the object: settings, start, stop, notes, timer, clocks, terms), XtMsmTrack.cpp (a track's start,
  stop and clock, the six Directions, SMOOTH, the two tables from rom.2), XtMsmPatch.cpp (the settings from a patch's store,
  xtMsmSettingsOf). Split by job: XtMsm.cpp alone was 311 lines.
  Check: xt_msm (XtMsmCheck.cpp, XtMsmCases.cpp; build.sh, fcheck): the firmware's object in the interpreter against the port,
  the object's bytes, its timer, the tempo ticks, the tone updates and the timer's messages after every event, the terms at each
  clock run and every destination and zone at each load. The presets read their store by the firmware's own routines. 55
  presets, 356,944 events, 489,446 terms; 300 random cases, 811,357 events, 1,340,938 terms; 0 different. Seeds 7, 31337,
  90210 at 3000 cases, 0 different. XT_MSM_FIRST=1 says what differs, with both objects' bytes; XT_MSM_STOP=1 stops at the
  first case that does. The first run found one: a Grid edited to fewer clocks mid-step makes SMOOTH run past ±63, and the
  coarse curve's lookup then reads on past its 64 entries, so the port reads 129 bytes there.
  Plants: msm_muts.txt through mutate_msm.py (PORT=msm, msmtrack, msmpatch in mutate.sh), 71, all caught; three first planted
  were equivalent within what can happen and were replaced (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md says which).

  is State (the top bit, NOTE or CTRL) and Note/CC, then Step 1-32 (0 REST, 1-127, 128 TIE). The firmware's routines that point a
  read object at the pattern (0x89479ad0, 0x89479b20) add 34 x the line, and the manual has 32 steps x 16 lines.
  First XtFiles.cpp (469 lines) split by job, xt_files' output byte for byte the same in every mode: XtPatchStore.cpp (the
  store's layout and the descriptors' reads; their tables now published once by an atomic pointer), XtFiles.cpp (a patch file into
  the store; xt_files' main and preset waves), XtPatchStoreCheck.cpp and XtWaveFileCheck.cpp (xt_files' patches and a wave's own
  chunks, XT_FILES_MAIN only). build.sh, CMakeLists.txt, fwsteps.sh and mutate.sh list the new files; mutate.sh has PORT=patchstore
  (xt_files) and PORT=tonestore (xt_tone) for XtPatchStore.cpp, and files_muts.txt's store plants moved to patchstore.
  Check: xt_files compares 4,727,296 values (565,248 more) and 590,912 against the firmware's reads (70,656 more), 0 different; its
  new pointed line: the four blocks with no zone found by the firmware's own routines and read by its getters (0x8947d310,

A close waits for its encodes, and Auto's classifier hears it v185 (2026-11-10): .claude/fix-plan.md, section 9, R1's 10 s.
  The destructor waited 10 s for the pool, and only the firmware heard its stop (v168). Auto's content classifier runs
  before any firmware, an autocorrelation per 512 samples, over 0.04 s a second of audio. Measured by VSynthEmuTeardownTest
  on the old code: a processor on Auto with a 360 s wave, closed 1 s in, took 15,538 ms, and JUCE printed "killing thread
  by force": the destructor gave up at 10 s, its status members were destroyed, and the pool killed the job's thread after
  its own 5 s. Now EncodeJob gives its work the stop, analyzeContent (an optional stop) reads no further frame once it's
  up, and the job starts no encode after it; the destructor waits with no deadline. The close in the classifier: 5-6 ms.
  Without the job's own check it was about 2 s, the port's SOLO encode of the wave, so the test's bound there is 0.5 s.
  The job's body moved whole to PluginEncodes.cpp as encodeWave (PluginProcessor.cpp is over the line limit; reencode
  keeps the parameter reads), its four-deep ternary on the Encode Type now a table. analyzeContent stayed in
  VariphraseEngine.cpp, its stop in the frame loop's own condition: moved, its typed pi would have had to change, and
  std::numbers::pi is 7 ulps off it. Not stopped still: the port's SOLO encoder after a stopped firmware SOLO encode (0.44 s
  for 140 s of audio, measured), and the deadline itself has no plant that can catch it now that nothing outlasts it.
  Plants (mutate_panel.py, panel_muts.txt): the classifier given no stop, 14,282 ms, and deaf to it, 14,169 ms, each a
  wait to the classifier's end with no thread killed; the encode started after the stop, 2,086 ms. 3 caught.

  code: xt_files, xt_tone, PluginTest and the tests read the firmware's disk image through it; the plugin doesn't) took a FAT16
  layout as its boot sector said, so a root directory past the image's end was read past it, clusters of no sectors made a
  looping FAT's chain never end, and a looping chain gave a file whatever size its entry claimed. Measured by
  VSynthEmuDiskImageTest on the old reader, on images it builds: the root past the end and the clusters of no sectors both
  taken, a looping 16 MB file read as 16,777,216 bytes, and the folder on no sectors not back in 3 s. Now open() refuses
  clusters of no sectors and a root past the end (the FATs are before it, so they are in the image too), and a chain takes
  only clusters whose data and FAT entry are in the image (clusterEnd), each once; the test holds 14/14 (a cluster with no
  FAT entry and one past the image added with the fix). The reader moved whole from XtFiles.cpp (558 lines) to
  XtDiskImage.cpp first, as the split rule says; the plant fat-chain followed (PORT=diskimage). fcheck as baseline, 664
  checks: xt_files and both xt_tone sections ran, the rest kept. WaveFileTest's deadline is TestChecks::within, shared.

The legacy engine's formant smoothing is each model's own v176 (2026-11-10): .claude/fix-plan.md, section 9. SourceFilterModel's
  downsampled formant analysis (Session 15) smoothed its autocorrelation from frame to frame in a function-local thread_local,
  so every model processed on one thread (each processor's legacy engine, which plays the loaded file on the LPC algorithm when
  there's no firmware image) smoothed into the same numbers, and a reset, the processor's on each note-on, kept them. Measured
  by VSynthEmuFormantTest on the old code: two engines, a vowel each with its formants shifted, block by block on one thread,
  differed from one alone in 47,359 of 48,128 samples, and one reset and played again from its first play in 29,134, both from
  the first frame on. Now the analysis is a class of its own, DownsampledFormants, holding the smoothing, restarted by
  SourceFilterModel::reset(); the test holds 3/3. Its Laguerre root finder was the one shiftFormants had, a copy since Session 15:
  one PolynomialRoots serves both, its search and polish one converge(). The analysis is split by its steps (code-shape's 50
  statements); the engine's set-up, by hand in the processor, EngineTests and the test, is one prepare(rate, block, algorithm,
  params). Null test (HEAD's OfflineRenderer against the new, two vowels, formant -12, -5, +7, +12, lpc and hybrid): hybrid
  bit for bit; lpc up to 9,943 of 48,128 samples apart, at most -147.0 dBFS. That is where the compiler fuses multiply-adds,
  nothing in the arithmetic: built with -ffp-contract=off the two are bit for bit (the default here is on; turning the
  downsampled path off, or the FIR's typed pi for std::numbers::pi, changed nothing). Under the house's -120 dBFS for a
  refactor, so taken. mutate_panel.py's new plants are in panel_muts.txt: formants-no-reset and formants-shared.

The ROM's tables written once v173 (2026-11-09): R7 of .claude/claude-packs-review.md. The ports' tables are the process's and
  every instance reads them without a lock, but xtSynthSetRom rewrote them, and XtFirmware's g_rom (a std::string), whenever an
  instance was given a file at a path the processor's loadRom hadn't loaded last: another image, or a copy of the same one.

  entry of the envelopes' time table 1 ms longer was taken ("ROM ready"), and a new instance's note on it sounded different.

  lock, an atomic ready flag stored only once every table is in (the key scales and gains were set after it before), the
  bytes' FNV-1a (xtfile::fnv, moved from xt_files to XtFiles.h) and the last path taken, which isn't read again (the rule the
  processor's loadRom kept, with its two statics; loadRom is gone). A file with the same bytes is taken and nothing written; a
  different one is refused, openRom saying "...: it isn't the image this host plays from, <name>; restart the host to change
  images". A junk file before any image is in use reads as before. Choosing between images in one process is no longer
  possible: the tables can't change under voices sounding, and the XT has one image.

Encodes that stop when their processor closes v168 (2026-11-09), no change to the sound: R1 of .claude/claude-packs-review.md.
  An encode runs the firmware in the interpreter on the processor's pool thread (a 30 s BACKING wave: 10.9 s), and a lambda job never
  heard removeAllJobs(true, ...) ask it to stop. So the destructor gave up after its 10 s, the members after encodePool_ (status_,
  statusLock_) were destroyed under the running job, which still called setStatus, and the pool killed its thread. Each encode is now
  an EncodeJob (PluginEncodes.cpp, addEncode) that turns the pool's exit signal into a flag the interpreter looks at once in 65536
  steps (Sh4StopScope in Sh4Interp.h; Sh4::call returns false, error "stopped"). Steps rise by 1 or 2 an instruction, a delay slot
  counting, so the look is at the low bits 0 or 1: a plain multiple can be stepped over for ever. With no scope held a call runs as
  before. The destructor is as it was, and its wait now ends in ms.
  Not done: the destructor still gives up after 10 s, so work after a stopped call that isn't the firmware's (Auto's classifier, the
  port's SOLO encoder, the resampler for a rate the converter won't take) would still reach the old fault past that: for the SOLO
  encoder, at 0.33 s per 30 s, about 15 minutes of audio (an estimate). R2, a wave's frames bounded by its data, is the rest. The wait
  wants no deadline: template-law refuses any edit of the destructor while its parameter list repeats the constructor's, and
  file-size-guard any growth of PluginProcessor.cpp, where the one list would go.
  Check: VSynthEmuTeardownTest (plugin/Tests/TeardownTest.cpp; TestAppDir.h, shared with PluginTest), in verify.sh: a 30 s BACKING
  encode, the processor closed at 1 s: 10765 ms before, 6 ms after; the next processor encodes as before.

The host's tempo v125 (2026-11-04): Clock Source, INTERNAL the Patch Tempo or HOST, which is the firmware's EXTERNAL with the host's transport for its MIDI clock (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The host's tempo, by the firmware's external clock"). XtSynth::hostClock from the processor's play head, before each host block; clockFollow 0x89471e30, clockIn the F8 0x894712f0, clockMs the end of the timer 0x894710e0, tempo10Now 150000/(+0x48).
  One clock to each 24th of a quarter the transport passes. A move of more than a clock is a locate: no clocks for what it passed. Stopped, or no position told, the clocks run on at its tempo, which is my choice, so that a note played on a stopped transport has them; no tempo told (the Standalone), the Patch Tempo stands.
  The tempo read is the firmware's: whole ms over six clocks, so it steps (120.5 BPM reads 120.0 or 120.9), 120 until the first count of six is done, and past its limits far off: over 250 BPM it reads 25.0, under 20 it reads 2.0. Clocks don't depend on it; a note's start does, an envelope's note value most.
  Parameter clockSource, a Clock box on the tempo row. CLI XT_HOST="bpm[,ppq[,moving]]", PluginTest TEST_HOST="bpm,ppq,moving". Default INTERNAL.
  Check: xt_tone draws Clock Source, the host's tempo, its block's length, locates, stops and stretches standing still; the firmware's own F8 and timer routines run on the port's clocks, with +0x40…+0x48 compared each block: 0 different at fcheck's 2000. A section runs an outside clock alone, on what no transport gives, and another holds the host's clocks to the transport's position. 3000 on seeds 2007, 90210, 55555, 777, 4242, 1234: 0 different; 31337 and 9: one case each past kSoloTol, a stereo SOLO wave on OSC 2, gone under v122's SOLO player in a scratch copy, where 31337, 9, 2024 are 0 different with the buffers exact. mutate_tone.py 413 of 413. A/B 4/4 identical to v118, PluginTest 4/4 at the defaults and on a play head in three settings.
  Not covered: a real XT or MIDI clock, how an F8 reaches the object from MIDI IN, the clock's phase within an F8's interval, a DAW (only PluginTest's play head).

The two branches together v124 (2026-11-04): v118 merged with v117, v119, v120 (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The two branches together").
  A note value takes no sum, and a sum stays within 0…127 so it cannot become one: withSum (0x894e00b0, 0x894df240). TRIP-R reads the pad now: +0x24 is the distance from the centre at each Y of a finger, 127 with none (0x894d8360); XtTripPad::radius. Full at the centre, 0 at the rim, so near 0 on the plugin's Trip knob.
  A pad's move brings no update, and a getter forms only its own group. So a key-off forms only the envelopes' groups (matrixRelease), not OSC Pitch, Level, the LFO depths, which XtSynth reads each tick; a legato forms all. mxMoved is gone. The whole of mutate_tone.py takes about 13 minutes now.

The chip is an ESP v123 (2026-11-04), no plugin change: JE-8086's ESP core (gearmulator, GPL-3.0) has the same word, ops, widths and memory controller as the XT's chip, and has all that v115 couldn't decode. With those facts an integer model of mine (build/tools/fx/esp4.cpp, not committed: licence) runs the XT's programs through the firmware's driver: no MFX silent or running away of 42, all reverbs decay, the EQ at +14.99, delays where the coefficients put them, the pitch shifter on pitch. Not compared with an XT yet. HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The chip is an ESP".

SOLO bit for bit v122 (2026-11-04): XtSoloVoice gives the firmware's output words bit for bit, mono and stereo (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "SOLO bit for bit"). The −83…−118 dB was the rounding: the windows' e + slope and the filter's sums in double, where the firmware truncates in single precision, so the errors have one sign and add up. Each alone is −85 to −87 dB on the vocal.
  Port: Chan holds the firmware's fields (start, tap, age int32, the rest float). Grains, filter and gain are plain float under FE_TOWARDZERO, as XtMbPlayer's, set in noteOn and renderXt, so nothing they call may use XtFloat's sums. renderXt writes the firmware's words, XtSynth and xt_analog call it, render is that × 2^-31. Also from the disassembly: the second tap is both grains or neither, the second weight is (wB + wA) − wA, the damping target is 0x3f999999 against note-on's 0x3f99999a. 12 ns a sample, as before.
  Check: solocmp counts words (31 cases, 17 new, 0 different); xt_tone has no tolerance and no SOLO exceptions (kSoloTol, soloBeside, Lo-Fi, Compressor, X-MOD, FM's lines); xt_analog has no limit. 0 different at fcheck's 2000, on seeds 3, 4, 777, 1007, 2007, 5007, 40000, 90210 at 10,000, and with 12 other wave pairs. mutate_tone.py 338 of 338. tone_muts.txt gains 128 in XtSoloVoice.cpp (PORT=tonesolo, and port solo for what only solocmp reaches). A/B: SOLO renders move −96.8 and −100.0 dB, BACKING and ENSEMBLE identical; `verify.sh`: fcheck as the new baseline; PluginTest 4/4 bit-identical, so the plugin's own build (-O3, LTO) gives the CLI's words, rounding mode included; engine tests and auval pass. Its exit is 1 only for the two A/B lines above.
  Closes the SOLO cases left open since v100. Left: a recording from the XT; bandcmp's 7.01e−46, which is the comparison's; the first-record rule (solo-wrap-first), not shown by any case.

Tempo sync and Time Trip v118 (2026-11-04): the Patch Tempo, note values on envelope times and LFO rates, each PCM OSC's Tempo Sync, the Time Trip pad, its Switch and Beat Keep (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "Tempo sync and Time Trip"). XtBeat (XtBeat.h, its bodies in XtMultiBandPlayer.cpp) is what a player does first in a block (0x8950c280); XtTripPad in XtOsc the pad; XtSynth::clockPeriod, clocksDue, clockTick the clock, on a block's start; oscBeat and OscVoice::chase letting go; lfoUpdates all five LFOs at any tone update.
  The tempo is read back from the clock's object with its tenths dropped, so 233 BPM reads as 233.4. An envelope keeps the tempo its note started with; an LFO and Tempo Sync follow the clock. Tempo Sync and Time Trip steer the player's walk by a factor, and its position is its own. Clock Source INTERNAL only: following the host's tempo is the user's call, not added (added in v125). MFX tempo stays out, with the effects.
  Parameters patchTempo, timeTrip (the pad as one plain knob: 0 lifted, one turn clockwise over 1..127, which is 48 steps, two quarter notes at the Original Tempo), and per OSC tempoSync, origTempo, timeTripSw, beatKeep. Hold, Sostenuto and the mode messages v117 (2026-11-04): CC 64, 66, 120, 121, 123…127, and how a controller reaches a tone (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "Hold, Sostenuto and the mode messages"). XtSynth::controller stores the value on the channel (below 96) and updates the voices for ANY number, as the plain handler does; keyRelease (0x894d58b0), voiceOff (0x894dc270), Voice::keyFlags (the note's +0x48: key, Hold, Sostenuto), relHeld (+0x2f).
  N (*K) is the part-and-channel state: +0x11c Hold, +0x11d Sostenuto, +0x120 its latch, +0xa the last release velocity. CC 84 is stored and nothing reads it. CC 5, 65, 68, 71…74 are the plain handler: nothing reads them unless the Matrix does.

The effects mapped v115 (2026-11-03), no plugin change: HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The effects". They run on a separate chip, not the SH-4: 768 steps per sample in two programs, A fixed (routing, sends, mix, EQ, Master, Output Gain), B Chorus (fixed), Reverb (350 words per type), MFX (307 per type); 8 int32 per frame each way by DMA (0xa4000000 out, 0xa4010000 back); 64K-sample-zoned delay memory.
  The SH-4 side (factory 0x894b43a0, each class's load, update, unload; Reverb's table at 0x8987ae3c; Chorus 0x894d3020) all runs in Sh4Interp with seven routines hooked (research/tools/fxmap.cpp). The chip's programs can't: there's no reference but a real XT, so "0 different in a harness" can't hold for them. That's the user's call.
  The word is decoded far enough that a throwaway model (research/tools/fxchip.cpp) gets Parametric EQ to ±15.00 dB and Stereo Delay's and Multi Tap's taps to the sample. Not finished: 319 of 10,489 active words undecoded, Chorus reads land wrong, Reverb diverges, most types not right yet.
  Proposed first version: XtFxChip with the fixed programs, Thru and the memory-free MFX types, converters in the interpreter, harness only. Whole order and unknowns in the section.
  Corrected: v114's g is ID 0x526, Out Gain for TG (default −24), not the Output Gain.

The SOLO encoder's stereo mode v113 (2026-11-03): xtSoloEncode(x, n, 2) now gives the records of 0x8951e120(3), the mode the XT's driver gives a stereo wave (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The SOLO encoder's stereo mode"). A band read far from its wave v108 (2026-11-03): seed 90210's case 9398 in xt_tone was no fault of XtSynth or a port (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "A band read far from its wave"). From two record lengths under a record's start, the backward
  repeat (0x8950f50a) can run until the position overflows to near 2^31, in the firmware and the port alike. Stereo band 0's read then aliases to memory below the band: past xt_tone's 256 KB of zeros, here on the wave's own vpdt, where the port reads 0.

  decoded, the Velocity Sens unit not), the Energy knob mapping.

WAV IMPORT v47 (2026-10-29): the XT's import converter (setup 0x8952dec0,
  16-bit driver 0x8952e4d0, resampler 0x8952dc60/dd20) run from rom.2 in the
  interpreter: xtFwImport. The plugin imports through it (sources wider
  than 16 bits keep their top 16 bits; 44.1 kHz files are not converted),
  and so does the engine's BACKING/ENSEMBLE path (VSE_SRC=sinc: the old
  windowed sinc). Suite 76.601 -> 76.765 (chord +0.3/+0.8/+2.5, drum ±0.2).
    xt_fw rom.2.bin import samp.bin <ch> <rate> out.bin     (int16 in and out)

AMP ENVELOPE v45 (2026-10-29): plugin/Source/XtEnvelope.cpp is the XT's
  envelope generator (HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md, "The envelope generator"):
  A/D/S/R 0..127 through the firmware's time table (ms) and sustain table,
  curve-2 attack and segment shapes, 10 ms ticks, per-sample follower.
  Validation against the firmware run in the SH-4 interpreter:
    clang++ -std=c++20 -O2 -DXT_ENV_MAIN plugin/Source/XtEnvelope.cpp plugin/Source/Sh4Interp.cpp -o xt_env
    xt_env rom.2.bin A D S R offTick ticks [curve]        (prints "identical" or the first difference)
  XtSynth CLI: XT_ADSR="a,d,s,r"; PluginTest: TEST_ADSR="a,d,s,r" (note-off halfway).
  Plugin parameters ampAttack/ampDecay/ampSustain/ampRelease replace "release" (ms).
  v46: applied as the XT's patch TVA (0x894f5360 update, 0x894f5720 render):
  linear ramps over 441 frames per update (shorter for short stages), block
  landing at 1/32.5, done at env stage 5 and gain <= 2^-16. The TVA's ramp
  confirms the 10 ms clock; the RECORDING_GUIDE takes are now an optional check.

      (processor end to end vs XtSynth at 48 kHz; exit 0 = bit-identical)

  VST3_AUTO_MANIFEST is off: JUCE's manifest helper cannot load the build-tree

Start a new session:
  Read ARCHITECTURE.md  (algorithm status, routing table, score history)
  Read RESEARCH_LOG.md  (session-by-session findings, open questions)

F0 ESTIMATION (v19b/c, SourceFilterModel::estimateF0):
  - NO pre-emphasis in estimateF0 (v19c): it boosts formants and on speech the
    F1-region ACF peaks (~600 Hz) beat the fundamental — F0 flapped 130<->600 Hz
    and pitch shifts rendered a fourth sharp. ACF pitch detection wants the
    fundamental dominant (the opposite of LPC analysis needs).
  - frame reaching ACF is Hann^2-tapered (windowed twice); winAcf_ (ACF of w^2,
    precomputed in prepare) unbiases it. Correction clamped at 2.0 (diverges at
    long lags -> spurious peaks).
  - OCTAVE GUARD: only integer subharmonics of the global max (lag/2,/3,/4,
    +-2 samples) qualify as shorter-lag candidates; a looser "any local max
    within 5%" rule picked formant sub-peaks at non-integer fractions.
  - Verified output fundamentals: sine cases 441.0 Hz (target 440); vocal
    pitch+7 190.5 Hz (target 195.9).

EXCITATION (v20): FLAT harmonic weighting in synthesiseExcitation (band-limited
  impulse train). DO NOT use 1/k sawtooth weighting: the spectral tilt is
  already carried once per path (pre-emph path: output de-emphasis filter;
  bypass path: LPC envelope of the untilted frame) — 1/k double-counts it,
  ~15 dB dark above 1 kHz. Tried and reverted (Session 15): output-domain
  energy normalization (de-emphasise synthFrame copy for the energy estimate,
  target original frame energy) — fixed the ~10 dB level deficit but scored
  31.9 vs 32.0; metric prefers the current pre-emph-domain normalization.

PV (v21, PhaseVocoder.cpp): inverted hops + synthesis window + identity
  phase locking + sign-corrected recursion. DO NOT use fractional/alternating
  analysis hops (breaks per-bin phase tracking). DO NOT revert the sign fix:
  forward FFT here is exp(+j); phases advance NEGATIVELY with delay.
  Tried and reverted: compression-side hop flip (analysis fixed / synthesis
  scaled down) scored 31.3 vs 31.9.

Routing (v23): LITE + (time-only OR pitch>-0.5st) → LPC resynthesis.
  BACKING time cases tested both ways: drum compression prefers LPC (+4/+5),
  drum extension + chord prefer PV (-6/-8) — kept on PV, too thin to split.

VOCAL FORMANT (v24, implemented): downsampled LPC fixes pole placement —
  measured output formants 492/1395/2139 Hz vs reference 463/1441/2227 (down-
  max, near-exact). The formant-similarity metric improves less than the
  physics because it scores TRAJECTORIES and per-frame jitter remains
  (autocorrelation smoothing alpha=0.7 helps; more aggressive smoothing or
  pole tracking could help further). V-Synth formant-knob mapping: upward
  saturates ~0.75x nominal, downward tracks nominal.

Tried and reverted (Session 15, late):
  - Down-shift formant calibration (0.85x and 0.75x nominal): both score
    worse than nominal despite per-formant reference ratios suggesting
    saturation (0.55-0.69) — the trajectory metric does not reward it.
  - PV onset phase reset (retried on the FIXED v21 PV — sign-corrected +
    identity locking): drum_2x +1.0 but vocal_time_2x -3.3 (false triggers
    on vowel onsets) and drum_halfspeed -1.3. Net negative; Session 10's
    conclusion stands even with correct phase propagation.

vocal_aah_formant_downmax (16.8) — EXHAUSTED EXPERIMENTS (do not retry):
  - down calibration 0.75x: 12.1; 0.85x: 12.1 — wants nominal ratio
  - cross-frame pole tracking (match-by-angle, 60/40 blend): flat (16.6)
  - split shift (hold poles >2.5 kHz on down-shift): 15.6
  - autocorrelation smoothing beyond alpha 0.7: worse
  Placement is measurably near-exact; whatever formant_sim penalises here
  (0.31) is not pole position, stability, or shift mapping. Next credible
  step would be analysing the metric itself on this pair, or more reference
  recordings to determine the V-Synth's actual downshift envelope over time.

PATENT FINDINGS (Session 15, research/PATENTS.md — READ THIS FIRST):
  US6421642B1 (Roland, filed 2000-05) IS VariPhrase. The real SOLO algorithm
  is PITCH-SYNCHRONOUS GRANULAR, not LPC:
    encode: cut phrase into ~1-pitch-period grains, store per-grain pitch
            (cwp) + syllable marks (= our event stamps)
    time   = rate of walking the grain list (tcv)
    pitch  = grain re-trigger period ppw = cwp x pitchRatio; TWO channels,
             half-cycle offset, triangular windows
    formant= grain READ VELOCITY fsv (resample grain content);
             window length = cwp/fsv CLAMPED <= ppw (explains our measured
             ~0.75x upward saturation mechanistically)
  US6564187B1: multiband subband time stretch — the ENSEMBLE/BACKING path.
  US6201175B1: band-wise sinusoidal modelling variant.
  Our LPC source-filter path approximates grain resynthesis; black-box was
  directionally right, mechanistically wrong.

Subband/granular A/B results (Session 15, do not re-run):
  - granular on ENSEMBLE: chord_time 31.5, chord_pitch 36.7 — worse than PV;
    pitch-sync cutting unsuitable for polyphony (why Roland filed US6564187).
  - subband on BACKING: drum_halfspeed 38.1 (worse than event-based 49.6 —
    trajectory resampling smears transients), drum_time_2x 54.9 (+3.0 vs
    event-based) — not adopted (one-case marginal; idea queued below).

FIRMWARE RE (started 2026-10-10, research/HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md — READ IT):
  Hardware is a V-SYNTH XT (rack). Firmware = XT System Update v2.02, kept in
  firmware/ (GITIGNORED — Roland copyright; never commit firmware, listings or
  copied tables). Unpacked blobs in firmware/unpacked/.
  - No encryption: zlib streams behind "MI004" headers. V-SynthXT.rom holds
    boot (0.09), kernel (2.02), V-Synth app (VAPP1, rom.2), VC-1 D-50 (rom.3),
    VC-2 Vocoder (rom.4).
  - Code = little-endian SH-4 (SH7750R, 266 MHz per XT service notes).
    V-Synth app loads at 0x89300000. Ghidra: SuperH4:LE:32:default.
  - XT specs: sound generator 32-bit FLOAT, effects 24-bit fixed — hypothesis:
    VariPhrase runs as SH-4 software (unconfirmed until disassembly).
  - App has "Encode Depth" parameter — not modelled by us.
  - rom.2+0x20ee00: 64-phase x 6-tap polyphase interpolation kernel (likely
    the grain/sample read resampler); +0x4eebb0: 129-tap lowpass FIR.
  - Ghidra pass 1 done (Ghidra 12.1.4 via brew; project firmware/ghidra/):
    5,671 functions. SH-4 DOES THE AUDIO DSP IN FLOAT, 32-sample blocks
    (0x894f8a70 = filter/drive chain). Decompile with FPSCR_PR=SZ=0.
    0x8952dc60 = 8-phase x 16-tap polyphase int16 resampler (import SRC).
  - Ghidra pass 2: V-SYNTH ENGINE RUNS AT 44.1 kHz; hardware SRC (CS8420)
    converts to 48k/96k on output ("SRC Output(44.1K) -> Output(48K)").
    Our 48 kHz references went 44.1k engine -> SRC -> 48k. Our engine runs
    at 48k: sample-count constants may be off by 1.088x. TEST: render at
    44.1k, resample to 48k, re-score.
  - Encoder starts via C++ virtual call (from 0x89440ae0 "NOW ENCODING") —
    not statically traceable. Decompiler needs per-range FPSCR mode
    tracking (fschg / lds FPSCR) before the audio cluster reads cleanly.
  - Ghidra pass 3: DECOMPILER FIXED (research/ghidra_scripts/README.md).
    Analog OSCs = asm at 0x89508800.. (phase step x 220/44100 -> 44.1k
    confirmed). 64x6 kernel = PCM playback interpolator (0x8950eb60,
    6 frac bits, int16, + one-pole LPF) — plain resampling, not grains.
  - PROBABLE VARIPHRASE PLAYER: 0x8950e1e0 (+ sibling 0x8950d890): two
    12.20 accumulators (phrase walk vs read rate), event-table walker
    0x8950c880 (= syllable/event marks), 0.6 limit in grain-length math.
  - PASS 4: GRAIN PLAYER DECODED (0x8950e1e0) — see HARDWARE_FIRMWARE.md.
    Records {u16 period in 1/16 smp (0xFFFF=continue), u16 info}; period
    interpolated to next record; read ptr at pitch rate jumps +-1 period to
    stay within one period of the phrase walk; output = linear crossfade of
    taps at Q and Q+L, w = 5*(clamp(lag/L,0.4,0.6)-0.4); 2-pt linear interp
    at 1/16 sample. No formant control (formant follows pitch) = the
    ENSEMBLE/BACKING player. SOLO/formant player not yet found.
  - PLAYER IMPLEMENTED: VariphraseEngine::periodJumpResynthOffline (exact
    transcription of the decoded behaviour; EngineTests 7). Results (v6):
    chord_pitch 52.3 (v29 PV 66.8), chord_time 52.2 (subband 72.9), drum
    pitch +2.3, drum time +1.0/-3.6/-0.5. Chord problem: a tempered chord
    is not periodic at any L — splice sidebands -30..-40 dB that the
    reference lacks (< -52 dB). With exact L the player reproduces all
    three transposed notes at reference levels. Available via env
    VSE_PERIODJUMP=B/D/T (offline experiment switch); BACKING decision
    waits on the drum re-recording check.
  - Session 16 late: grain periods now SUB-SAMPLE (parabolic ACF peak,
    quantised to 1/16 sample) and grain starts accumulate fractionally,
    as in the firmware's contiguous records. Score-neutral (68.42 ->
    68.40, all cases within +-0.6); kept as firmware-faithful. The
    period-jump test's "97 Hz subharmonic" was an ACF-estimator tie, not
    the player (-119 dB on a perfect tone).
  - v30: ENSEMBLE pitch -> SUBBAND (Session 15's rejection was metric-v4
    noise). chord_pitch 66.8 -> 71.6. The PV had been DROPPING the
    transposed C and E of the chord (only G + a stray unshifted 523 Hz).
  - MANUAL (Vsynth_software/V-SYNTH_XT_OM.pdf, gitignored): LITE + SOLO
    have formant; BACKING/ENSEMBLE ignore it (v29 correct). Formant knob
    is -63..+63, test "max" = +-63. SOLO has Energy (fundamental emphasis)
    and robot voice; SOLO can make octave errors (pitch detection).
  - PASS 5: 0x8950db20 = grain RE-TRIGGER player (e760 scheduler over a
    16-bit grain-length list, 1/16-sample linear reads) + optional
    30-partial additive layer + 3 biquads -> leading SOLO candidate.
    Next: read e760/f400/f4c0 fully; find where f(+0x70)*f(+0x9c) rates
    are computed (formant -63..+63 mapping).
  - PASS 6: PLAYER ROUTING FOUND (0x894f3660, mode field voice+0x80):
    modes 1/6 -> cd90/d0f0/d380 = TWO-CHANNEL GRAINS WITH READ VELOCITY
    (formant) = SOLO(+LITE?); mode 5 -> db20 = 5 bands x 6 sinusoids
    (US6564187) = ENSEMBLE?; mode 3 -> e6e0 = 5-band decimated PCM grains
    = BACKING?; mode 8 -> e1e0 period-jump = ?. Window-param maths in
    0x8950cde0 (1.2, 1.9 cap, 3.0962/4.4208 cubic) — formant saturation?
  - PASS 7: converters decoded. Pitch = 2^(clamp(cents,+-6000)/1200);
    time clamp +-5; FORMANT RATIO = clamp(2^x, 0.5, 2.0), x in octaves
    (base + env*depth + lfo*depth). Depth knobs: sign(k)*(k/63)^2*range.
    S15 asymmetry (down = 0.5, up ~1.68) consistent with clamp + SOLO
    window mechanics (0x8950cde0), not knob mapping — confirm next.
  - PASS 8 / v31-v32: SOLO ENGINE DECODED AND IMPLEMENTED
    (soloFirmwareResynthOffline, default SOLO path; VSE_SOLO=patent for the
    old one). Two alternating channels, triangle windows over 2*wl,
    velocity = formant, patent clamp, 2nd tap one period later weighted by
    progress through the record, highpass SVF (Energy). Epoch-aligned cuts
    (snap to + peak) are part of the design: fw +2.1 vocal, patent -7.3.
    Vocal: fmt_down 62.7, fmt_up4 58.0, fmt_up 65.5, pitch_down 70.6,
    pitch_up 63.9, time_2x 71.8, time_half 66.7. Energy (VSE_ENERGY=g)
    decoded but scaling unknown; off by default.
  - V-TRAINER (VP-9000 PC encoder, vpenc.dll from the V-Producer CD):
    encode types 1/3/5 = SOLO/BACKING/ENSEMBLE = firmware modes 1/3/5
    (routing now pinned). Templates (pmin 22, pmax 2000 Hz, voicing

    encodeGrainCutsVTrainer (VSE_ENC=vt): 64.2 vs 65.6 default -> not

  - V-TRAINER LIVE ORACLE (Session 16, 2026-10-14; research/VPENC_ABI.md §5):

    player): 74.61 -> 78.04; + VSE_ANCHOR 77.86. Isolation: phase shift of

    bug: the vocal onset has two pulses per period and the taller one is
    the secondary for 4 cycles; peak-snap locked onto it and slipped half
    a period later (267/292-sample records). v34 rule: run-start mark at
    the max-energy p/6 window over the whole first period (+ voiced
    lead-in record), later marks snap to the energy epoch within +-p/4.
    vs XT marks: median -1.6 samples, 78% within 5, 0 slips (v33: -14.9,
    2%, 10 slips). Vocal 77.40; suite 73.9; non-vocal bit-identical.
    VSE_EPOCH=peak restores v33. No feature tracks XT marks tighter than
    IQR ~3.7 samples (consistent with free stepping).
    V-Trainer vs XT: V-Trainer (vpfl 0101) on the old capture input put
    marks ~114 after the positive peak with 137 unvoiced records; the XT
    puts them ~15 after the peak, all voiced. Capture is same polarity
    (r = 0.93) -> the two encoders likely DIFFER. Decisive test: V-Trainer

  - v35 ENCODER (2026-10-18): estimateLocalPeriod octave guard now checks
    submultiples k = 8..2 (was k = 2): on the pure sine every multiple of

    integer version stuttered 100/101. Sine (as recorded, SOLO) 36.2 ->
    71.8 -> 74.6; vocal 77.40 -> 77.41; everything else bit-identical.
  - RETRACTED (2026-10-18): a "0.93 formant warp in every vocal ref"
    was my analysis bug — refs are 48 kHz, raw/renders 44.1 kHz, and the
    envelope script compared FFT bins by index (44.1/48 = 0.919). LPC
    formants (sr-aware) of the refs match raw. Always compare in Hz.
  - PATCH FILES (.p00, 2026-10-22, partial): Roland SVD container ("SVD0",
    "PAT" chunk, data from byte 0x20, 5370 B). Data is a BIT-PACKED stream
    of raw SysEx values in address-map order (research/V-Synth_Owners_
    Manual.txt ~10567). Patch name = 12 x 7-bit chars at bit 264 (verified
    on presets). Zone blocks repeat: oscillator 624 bits x16 (zone 1 near
    bit 2098), envelope 896 x16 (from ~12071), 272 x16 (from ~33324).
    Field width ~ bit_length(max value) (keyfollow 44-84 -> 7 bits, not the
    manual's 6), but osc sums to 611 not 624, so a sequential decode drifts.
    Goal: read OSC1 Formant / Formant KF (0x39/0x3A) / Fmt LFO / Fmt Env
    depth of the sine patch. Cheapest fix: two saves of one patch differing
    in ONE known parameter -> bit diff pins the field. Or read the XT screen.
  - On "use the firmware directly": we transcribe behaviour + constants
    into our own code (safe). Running Roland's machine code under an SH-4
    emulator (JE-8086 style, user-supplied ROM, never distributed) is a
    possible LOCAL reference renderer — large effort, legally grey for a
    plugin. Do not paste decompiled code into the repo.

44.1 kHz EXPERIMENT (Session 16, 2026-10-10) — NO MEASURABLE DIFFERENCE:
  Method: resample 48k dry inputs (vocal/chord/drum) to 44.1k, render,
  compare.py resamples output to 48k (sine inputs were already 44.1k).
  First run (metric v4) looked like chord_pitch +22.8 / vocal -5..-16 —
  BOTH were bugs, not signal:
  - chord +22.8 = METRIC BUG (fixed -> metric v5, below).
  - vocal drops = ENGINE BUG: granular encoder used fixed sample counts
    (1024-sample ACF window, 256-sample unvoiced hop). Now defined in time
    (grainAcfWindow / grainUnvoicedHop, = 1024/256 at 48k; 48k renders
    bit-identical). Also matters for the real-time plugin at any host rate.
  After both fixes (metric v5): 48k engine 68.08, 44.1k engine 68.14.
  The references cannot distinguish the two rates at current fidelity.
  Keep rendering at the input's native rate.

METRIC v5 (Session 16): spectral_similarity level-matches by RMS ratio
  (_energy_gain) instead of the least-squares _optimal_gain. The LS gain is
  PHASE-SENSITIVE: on resynthesized content it swung -0.57..+0.68 with ms
  shifts and clipped at 0.25 (chord_pitch spectral 0.35 <-> 0.67 from a
  20 ms offset). v5 spread under 0-20 ms offsets <= 0.034 on all 20 cases.
  v5 scores are much higher than v4 and NOT comparable. v28 under v5 = 68.1.
  Known v5 limits (dry-input floor test): spectral term is envelope-
  dominated — at n_fft 512-2048 low-voice harmonics are unresolved, so
  formant-preserving pitch shifts score close to the UNSHIFTED dry input
  (vocal_pitch_down12st: dry 0.825 > render 0.782). Pitch cases need a
  pitch-accuracy term. Dry also beats render on chord_formant (0.935 vs
  0.785) — consistent with ENSEMBLE ignoring the formant knob: our chord
  formant render is genuinely wrong (should be ~passthrough).
  Floor numbers: run dry passthrough vs each ref (see Session 16 log).

METRIC v6 (Session 16): adds PITCH term harmonic_similarity — Pearson
  correlation of the envelope-removed long-term spectrum (n_fft 16384,
  50-4000 Hz): the harmonic comb, independent of formants and timing.
  Weights (spectral/pitch/formant/transient/snr):
    speech .35/.20/.20/.15/.10  tonal .50/.30/0/.10/.10
    percussive .45/0/0/.45/.10 (unchanged — drum comb is meaningless)
  Render-vs-dry margin on pitch cases: v5 +11.5 -> v6 +20.8 (vocal
  pitch_down12st now beats dry: +3.6, was -3.5). pYIN median F0 was tried
  first and rejected: it octave-jumped on a -36 dB subharmonic in the sine
  formant renders (those renders are NOT an octave off — fundamental is
  correct).
  Remaining render<dry cases: drum_pitch (-7.3, see below),
  vocal_time_halfspeed (-6.9).

v29 (Session 16): ENSEMBLE/BACKING ignore the formant knob
  (VariphraseEngine::Impl::hybridParams zeroes formantShift for those
  encode types; owner's manual + chord ref ~ dry input). chord_formant
  49.7 -> 81.5 (v6); only render that changed. Outputs:
  analysis/plugin_outputs/hybrid_v29, results analysis/results/hybrid_v29.

DRUM REFERENCES SUSPECT (Session 16): drum_hit_pitch_up7st reference has
  the SAME spectral peaks as the dry sample (62/92/219/495/669 Hz) — not
  transposed. drum_hit_time_2x reference peaks sit ~3.5 st BELOW dry.
  Either recorded from a different key than the original, or settings
  not as named. Check RECORDING_GUIDE / re-record before tuning drums.

TRUE v28 BASELINE = 52.5 (not 51.8): the committed hybrid_v18d renders of
  vocal_aah_formant_downmax (35.7) and vocal_aah_pitch_up7st (37.5) were
  STALE (pre-granular). Current source renders them 38.8 / 49.0. Other 18
  cases bit-identical.

BUILD NOTE (macOS 27 host): /usr/bin/clang++ fails to link (CLT SDK 27 tbd
  "unknown architecture"). Build with Xcode's toolchain:
    TC=/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Developer/Toolchains/XcodeDefault.xctoolchain/usr/bin
    SDK=/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Developer/Platforms/MacOSX.platform/Developer/SDKs/MacOSX.sdk
    env -u SDKROOT $TC/clang++ -isysroot $SDK -std=c++20 -O2 -DOFFLINE_RENDERER_MAIN \
      OfflineRenderer.cpp VariphraseEngine.cpp PhaseVocoder.cpp SourceFilterModel.cpp XtSoloEncoder.cpp \
      XtSoloVoice.cpp XtMultiBandPlayer.cpp Sh4Interp.cpp XtFirmware.cpp -o ...
PYTHON: system python has no numpy. Use the project venv: .venv/bin/python
  (created Session 16 from analysis/requirements.txt; .venv/ gitignored).
  Other Roland software (V-Producer, V-Synth Librarian) in Vsynth_software/
  (gitignored).
